4.4
Local equations at Non-Borel ideals

Let
\lambda_{1311}
be the 3D partition
\big((1)\subset(3,1,1)\big)
:

and

I_{1311}=(X_{1}^{3},X_{1}X_{2},X_{1}X_{3},X_{2}^{3},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2}).

This is the unique non-Borel monomial ideal of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
of colength
6
.

First we show that Lemma
4.3
and the result in
4.3.3
give us the local structure of
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{1311}})
at 0.
Let
\epsilon\in\Bbbk
be a nonzero element. A linear transform
X_{1}\mapsto X_{1}+\epsilon X_{2}
transforms
I
to the ideal

\displaystyle J_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(X_{1}^{3}+3\epsilon X_{1}^{2}X_{2}+3\epsilon^{2}X_{1}X_{2}^{2}+X_{2}^{3},X_{1}X_{2}+\epsilon X_{2}^{2},X_{1}X_{3}+\epsilon X_{2}X_{3},X_{2}^{3},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(X_{1}^{3},X_{1}X_{2}+\epsilon X_{2}^{2},X_{1}X_{3},X_{2}^{3},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2}).

Since
\epsilon\neq 0
, the resulted ideal is equal to

\displaystyle(X_{1}^{3},X_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}X_{1}X_{2},X_{1}X_{3},X_{2}^{3},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2})

(57)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(X_{1}^{3},X_{1}X_{2}^{2},X_{1}X_{3},X_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}X_{1}X_{2},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(X_{1}^{3},X_{1}^{2}X_{2},X_{1}X_{3},X_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}X_{1}X_{2},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2}).

So it lies in the Haiman neighborhood of
I_{132}
, with Haiman coordinate
c_{110}^{020}=-\frac{1}{\epsilon}
. Since the extra dimension of
\mathrm{Hilb}^{6}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
at
Z_{I_{1311}}
is 6, the extra dimension at
Z_{I^{\prime}_{1311}}
is also 6. But we have seen that the whole Haiman neighborhood of
I_{132}
is a trivial affine fibration over the cone
\widehat{G}(2,6)
. So
I^{\prime}_{1311}
lies in the fiber (
\cong\mathbb{A}_{\Bbbk}^{6}
) over the vertex of the cone. Thus we obtain that, there is an open neighborhood of
Z_{I_{1311}}
that is isomorphic to an open subset of
\widehat{G}(2,6)\times\mathbb{A}^{9}
. This is NOT enough for the computation of the equivariant Hilbert function of
A_{\lambda_{1311}}
: the above obtained isomorphism is not equivariant.

I cannot find a conceptual proof of such an equivariant isomorphism, but only apply the algorithm and the trick of change of variables in the previous sections.
The algorithm
4.21
does not perform for non-Borel monomial ideals as well as for the Borel ideals. For example, the Haiman equations implies, for
i\in\lambda_{1311}
,

\displaystyle c_{i}^{210}=\frac{1}{(1-c_{020}^{110}c_{200}^{110})}\big(c_{000}^{110}c_{i}^{100}+c_{100}^{110}c_{i}^{200}+c_{200}^{110}c_{i}^{300}+c_{010}^{110}c_{i}^{110}+c_{001}^{110}c_{i}^{101}

\displaystyle+c_{020}^{110}(c_{000}^{110}c_{i}^{010}+c_{010}^{110}c_{i}^{020}+c_{020}^{110}c_{i}^{030}+c_{100}^{110}c_{i}^{110}+c_{001}^{110}c_{i}^{011})\big),

and symmetrically

\displaystyle c_{i}^{120}=\frac{1}{(1-c_{020}^{110}c_{200}^{110})}\big(c_{000}^{110}c_{i}^{010}+c_{010}^{110}c_{i}^{020}+c_{020}^{110}c_{i}^{030}+c_{100}^{110}c_{i}^{110}+c_{001}^{110}c_{i}^{011}

\displaystyle+c_{200}^{110}(c_{000}^{110}c_{i}^{100}+c_{100}^{110}c_{i}^{200}+c_{200}^{110}c_{i}^{300}+c_{010}^{110}c_{i}^{110}+c_{001}^{110}c_{i}^{101})\big).

This is typical for non-Borel monomial ideals: to eliminate the variables we need to allow the fractions. The algorithm
4.21
gives a set of equations in
x_{1},\dots,x_{29}
, where

\displaystyle c_{2,0,0}^{2,1,0}\mapsto x_{1},\ c_{0,2,0}^{2,1,0}\mapsto x_{2},\ c_{0,1,0}^{2,1,0}\mapsto x_{3},\ c_{0,0,1}^{2,1,0}\mapsto x_{4},\ c_{1,0,0}^{1,1,0}\mapsto x_{5},\ c_{1,0,0}^{1,0,1}\mapsto x_{6},

\displaystyle c_{1,0,0}^{3,0,0}\mapsto x_{7},\ c_{1,0,0}^{0,0,2}\mapsto x_{8},\ c_{2,0,0}^{1,1,0}\mapsto x_{9},\ c_{2,0,0}^{1,0,1}\mapsto x_{10},\ c_{2,0,0}^{3,0,0}\mapsto x_{11},\ c_{2,0,0}^{0,1,1}\mapsto x_{12},

\displaystyle c_{2,0,0}^{0,3,0}\mapsto x_{13},\ c_{2,0,0}^{0,0,2}\mapsto x_{14},\ c_{0,1,0}^{1,1,0}\mapsto x_{15},\ c_{0,1,0}^{0,1,1}\mapsto x_{16},\ c_{0,1,0}^{0,3,0}\mapsto x_{17},\ c_{0,2,0}^{1,1,0}\mapsto x_{18},

\displaystyle c_{0,2,0}^{1,0,1}\mapsto x_{19},\ c_{0,2,0}^{3,0,0}\mapsto x_{20},\ c_{0,2,0}^{0,1,1}\mapsto x_{21},\ c_{0,2,0}^{0,3,0}\mapsto x_{22},\ c_{0,2,0}^{0,0,2}\mapsto x_{23},\ c_{0,0,1}^{1,1,0}\mapsto x_{24},

\displaystyle c_{0,0,1}^{1,0,1}\mapsto x_{25},\ c_{0,0,1}^{3,0,0}\mapsto x_{26},\ c_{0,0,1}^{0,1,1}\mapsto x_{27},\ c_{0,0,1}^{0,3,0}\mapsto x_{28},\ c_{0,0,1}^{0,0,2}\mapsto x_{29}.

The number of variables is larger than the embedded dimension 24. There are equations of minDegree 1. Using them we eliminate
x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{8}
, allowing inverting

(1-x_{9}x_{18})(1-2x_{9}x_{18}).

For
\lambda_{1311}
we can find a fractional change of variables, more precisely an automorphism of the ring

\Bbbk[x_{5},x_{6},x_{7},x_{9},\dots,x_{29}]\Big[\frac{1}{(1-x_{18}x_{9})(1-2x_{18}x_{9})(1-2x_{18}x_{9}-x_{18}^{2}x_{9}^{2})(1-3x_{18}x_{9}+x_{18}^{2}x_{9}^{2})}\Big],

(58)

such that the Haiman ideal is transformed to the ideal

\displaystyle J_{1321}=(x_{12}x_{15}+x_{13}x_{19}+x_{14}x_{24},\ x_{12}x_{20}+x_{23}x_{24}+x_{19}x_{5},\ -x_{16}x_{24}-x_{12}x_{26}+x_{19}x_{28},

\displaystyle-x_{14}x_{20}+x_{15}x_{23}+x_{19}x_{29},\ x_{20}x_{28}+x_{26}x_{5}+x_{24}x_{7},\ x_{17}x_{24}-x_{13}x_{26}-x_{15}x_{28},

\displaystyle-x_{16}x_{20}+x_{23}x_{26}-x_{19}x_{7},\ -x_{13}x_{23}+x_{12}x_{29}+x_{14}x_{5},\ x_{13}x_{20}+x_{24}x_{29}-x_{15}x_{5},

\displaystyle x_{17}x_{20}+x_{26}x_{29}+x_{15}x_{7},\ x_{13}x_{16}+x_{12}x_{17}+x_{14}x_{28},\ x_{15}x_{16}-x_{17}x_{19}-x_{14}x_{26},

\displaystyle x_{23}x_{28}+x_{16}x_{5}-x_{12}x_{7},\ x_{17}x_{23}+x_{16}x_{29}+x_{14}x_{7},\ x_{28}x_{29}-x_{17}x_{5}-x_{13}x_{7}).

For the details see Appendix
B
.
Finally the map

\displaystyle x_{5}\mapsto p_{1,3},\ x_{7}\mapsto p_{1,2},\ x_{12}\mapsto p_{3,4},\ x_{13}\mapsto p_{0,3},\ x_{14}\mapsto p_{0,4},\ x_{15}\mapsto p_{0,5},

\displaystyle x_{16}\mapsto p_{2,4},\ x_{17}\mapsto-p_{0,2},\ x_{19}\mapsto p_{4,5},\ x_{20}\mapsto p_{1,5},\ x_{23}\mapsto p_{1,4},\ x_{24}\mapsto-p_{3,5},

\displaystyle x_{26}\mapsto p_{2,5},\ x_{28}\mapsto-p_{2,3},\ x_{29}\mapsto p_{0,1}.

transforms the ideal
J_{1321}
to the Plücker ideal (
4.3.1
).

There are three non-Borel monomial ideals of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
of colength
7
, see Appendix
C
.
However, such an explicit fractional change of variables is too difficult to find in these cases. We can only state the following conjecture.

We say that a torus action on the cone
\widehat{G}(2,6)
is
standard
if it is induced by an action on the tangent space at the vertex that preserves the Plücker ideal.

Conjecture 4.23
.

Let
I_{\lambda}
be a non-Borel monomial ideal of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
with extra dimension 6. Then there is a
T
-stable open subset
U
of
0\in\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{A}_{\lambda})
and an equivariant open immersion
f:U\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}(2,6)\times\mathbb{A}^{9}

where
\widehat{G}(2,6)
is equipped with a standard
T
-action.

Once we know the existence of such an equivariant isomorphism, to compute the equivariant Hilbert function of
\mathbb{A}_{\lambda}
, it suffices to determine the standard action on
\widehat{G}(2,6)
and the action on
\mathbb{A}^{9}
induced by
f
. We do this by considering only the degree
\leq 2
terms of the Haiman equations when we eliminate the variables. That is, we modify Algorithm
4.21
by
cutting off
the degree
\geq 3
monomials appearing in the resulted equations in every step. In this way, assuming Conjecture
4.23
, we compute the equivariant Hilbert functions of
A_{\lambda_{1411}}
,
A_{\lambda_{2311}}
and
A_{\lambda_{11311}}
in Appendix
C
.

4.5
Phenomenology of the local structures

In this subsection we discuss several conjectures observed from the computations in the previous sections and the appendices
A

and
C
.

4.5.1
Critical locus

Conjecture 4.24
.

Let
\lambda
be a 3-dimensional partition of
n
such that
I_{\lambda}
is a Borel ideal of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
. Then there exists a regular function
F_{\lambda}
on the tangent space of
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
at
Z_{I}
such that the Haiman neighborhood
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda})
is isomorphic to the critical locus of
F_{\lambda}
.

It is known (e.g.
[
BBS13
, Proposition 3.1]
) that
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda})
is a critical locus in a regular scheme of dimension larger than the embedded dimension of
A_{\lambda}
.

By the results in Sections
4.3.1
-
4.3.4
,
4.4
, and appendices A and B, the conjecture is true for
n\leq 7
. By Theorem
4.16
it holds for pyramids.
By Lemma
4.3
,
the conjecture implies that for any ideal
I
of colength
n
of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
, there exists a regular function
F
on the tangent space of
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
at
Z_{I}
such that an open neighborhood of
Z_{I}
is isomorphic to an open neighborhood of the critical locus of
F
.

Such statements are not true in dimension
>3
.

4.5.2
Monomial ideals with extra dimension 6

By abuse of notation we say a monomial ideal
I
of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
of finite colength
n
is a monomial ideal with extra dimension
d
if the extra dimension of
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
at
Z_{I}
equals
d
. As we have noted after Theorem
4.1
, if
I
is a monomial ideal with extra dimension 0, then
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
is smooth at
Z_{I}
. Inspired by the results in the previous sections and the appendices, and the verifications on computers, we have the following conjecture.

Conjecture 4.25
.

For
n\geq 4
,
the smallest nonzero extra dimension of points on
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
is 6.

As one sees in Sections
4.3.1
-
4.3.3
,
4.4
, and the appendices, the monomial ideals with extra dimension equal to 6 have similar shapes. A typical case of ideals of this shape is

I_{1}=(X_{1}^{n_{1}},X_{1}X_{2},X_{1}X_{3},X_{2}^{n_{2}},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{n_{3}}),

where
n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}\geq 2
. Using Lemma
4.14
, an easy counting shows that the extra dimension of
I_{1}
is 6. Graphically its corresponding 3-dimensional partition looks like

This picture inspires the following name.

Definition 4.26
.

A monomial ideal
I
of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
is called a
tripod
ideal, if
I
has a set of minimal generators of the form

X_{1}^{a},X_{1}^{b}X_{2}^{c},X_{1}^{d}X_{3}^{e},X_{2}^{f},X_{2}^{g}X_{3}^{h},X_{3}^{i}.

Note that the condition
minimal
puts restrictions on these exponents.

There exist monomial ideals
I
of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
with extra dimension 6 that are not tripod ideals.
For example,

I_{2}=(X_{1}^{3},X_{1}^{2}X_{2},X_{1}X_{2}^{2},X_{1}X_{2}X_{3},X_{2}^{3},X_{2}^{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2}),\ Z_{I_{2}}\in\mathrm{Hilb}^{10}(\mathbb{A}^{3}).

The corresponding 3-dimensional partition is

But
I_{2}
is not a Borel ideal. In fact by checking monomial ideals of colength
\leq 25
, we make the following conjecture.

Conjecture 4.27
.

A Borel ideal of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
with extra dimension 6 is a tripod ideal.

Conversely, let us consider which tripod ideals have extra dimension equal to 6. First we select the Borel tripod ideals.

Lemma 4.28
.

A tripod ideal
I=(X_{1}^{a},X_{1}^{b}X_{2}^{c},X_{1}^{d}X_{3}^{e},X_{2}^{f},X_{2}^{g}X_{3}^{h},X_{3}^{i})
is Borel fixed in the lexicographic order
X_{1}\succ X_{2}\succ X_{3}
if and only if

c=e=g=h=1,\ f=i=2.

The proof is straightforward from Definition
4.4
(ii). Then we make the following conjecture.

Conjecture 4.29
.

A Borel ideal of the form
I=(X_{1}^{a},X_{1}^{b}X_{2},X_{1}^{d}X_{3},X_{2}^{2},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2})
has extra dimension = 6 if and only if at least one of
b
and
d
is equal to
1
or
a-1
.

We have checked it for the ideals of this form of colength
\leq 100
.

4.5.3
Types of singularities

Proposition 4.30
.

Let
z
be a point on
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
. For
n\leq 7
, if the embedded dimension at
z
is
3n+6
, then there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
z
and an open immersion
U\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}(2,6)\times\mathbb{A}^{3n-9}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.3
, we need only consider the Haiman neighborhoods of
Z_{I}
where
I
runs over the Borel ideals of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
of colength
\leq 7
. Then by Example
4.6
, the results follow from Sections
4.3.1
-
4.3.3
, Appendices
A.1
-
A.4
, and the proof of Proposition
6.2
(also Appendix
D
).
∎

Conjecture 4.31
.

Let
z
be a point on
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
. If the embedded dimension at
z
is
3n+6
, then
z
has an open neighborhood which is isomorphic to an open subset of a trivial affine fibration over the cone
\widehat{G}(2,6)
.

In short, these singular points are of the same type, i.e. their germs can be transformed to each other by a chain of smooth morphisms. Note that when
n
is sufficiently large, e.g.
n\geq 78
, the Hilbert scheme
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
is reducible. Conjecture
4.31
might need to be modified for the points not lying in the main component. I have no clue about this. But since the monomial ideals all lie on the main component (Theorem
4.1
), Conjecture
4.31
should be true for them.

Also, the monomial ideals
I_{1321}
in Section
4.3.4
and
I_{2321}
in Appendix
A.5
are ideals with extra dimension 8, and have similar shapes of partitions. By the results of Section
4.3.4
and Appendix
A.5
, they correspond to the same type of singularities on
\mathrm{Hilb}^{7}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
and
\mathrm{Hilb}^{8}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
, respectively.

In general we make the following conjecture.

Conjecture 4.32
.

Let
\lambda_{1}
,
\lambda_{2}
be two
r
-dimensional partitions of length
l_{1}
,
l_{2}
respectively. Suppose
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
have similar shapes and

\mathrm{ex.dim}_{I_{\lambda_{1}}}\mathrm{Hilb}^{l_{1}}(\mathbb{A}^{r})=\mathrm{ex.dim}_{I_{\lambda_{2}}}\mathrm{Hilb}^{l_{2}}(\mathbb{A}^{r}),

then
\mathrm{Hilb}^{l_{1}}(\mathbb{A}^{3})_{Z_{I_{\lambda_{1}}}}
and
\mathrm{Hilb}^{l_{2}}(\mathbb{A}^{3})_{Z_{I_{\lambda_{2}}}}
are singularities of the same type.

The
shape
of a partition
\lambda
, roughly means the relative positions among the minimal lattices of the glove of
\lambda
, or equivalently among the lattices of the exponents of the minimal monomial generators of the ideal
I_{\lambda}
. For example, the minimal lattices of the glove of a tripod partition look like the left handside graph of (
59
):

(59)

As I have checked on
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
for
n\leq 20
, the minimal lattices of the glove of a partition, which corresponds to Borel ideals with extra dimension 8, looks like the right handside graph of (
59
). Note that in both graphs, the collinear vertices do not indicate that the corresponding lattices in
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
are collinear.

So for two partitions to have similar shapes, it is necessary that the corresponding monomial ideals have the same number of minimal monomial generators. But this condition is of course not sufficient. For example, plane partitions embedded into
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
always correspond to smooth points (Lemma
4.15
), but the corresponding ideals can have an arbitrary number of minimal monomial generators. I expected a stronger necessary condition for two 3D partitions
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
to have similar shapes: there exists a 3D partition
\lambda
such that
\lambda\subset\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda\subset\lambda_{2}
, and
I_{\lambda}
,
I_{\lambda_{1}}
,
I_{\lambda_{2}}
have the same number of minimal monomial generators.

I cannot formulate Conjecture
4.32
in a precise way for I do not have enough examples to make precise what
similar shapes
mean. In fact in our examples above, having the same extra dimension suffices. But I still feel that in the general cases we need a certain condition on the shapes of the
r
-dimensional partitions. A reason is that, as we have seen in Section
4.5.2
, when the extra dimension is small the shape of the partition has few choices, at least the Borel ones. The first example of partitions that do not have similar shapes while the corresponding ideals have the same extra dimension, is

which correspond to Borel ideals with extra dimension 12.

Question 4.33
.

Are the singularities on
\mathrm{Hilb}^{8}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
,
\mathrm{Hilb}^{9}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
,
\mathrm{Hilb}^{12}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
, and
\mathrm{Hilb}^{16}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
, corresponding to the above 3-dimensional partitions respectively, of the same type?

5  Euler characteristics of tautological sheaves

5.1
Hilbert series of tripod singularities

The singularities of
A_{\lambda}
in Sections
4.3.1
,
4.3.2
,
4.3.3
,
4.4
,
A.1

A.2
,
A.3
,
A.4
,
C.1
,
C.2
, and
C.3
have the same singularity type (in the sense of modulo the smooth equivalence). We compute their equivariant Hilbert functions in a uniform way. Let

R_{G(2,6)}=\Bbbk[p_{i,j}]_{0\leq i<j\leq 5}.

Then the ring

S_{G(2,6)}=R_{G(2,6)}/I_{G(2,6)}

is the coordinate ring of the cone
\widehat{G}(2,6)
, where
I_{G(2,6)}
is the Plücker ideal (
4.3.1
). There is an action of
T_{1}=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{6}
on
S_{G(2,6)}
, with weights

\mathbf{w}(p_{i,j})=\epsilon_{i}+\epsilon_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{6},\ 0\leq i\neq j\leq 5,

(60)

where
\{\epsilon_{i}\}_{0\leq i\leq 5}
is a basis of
\mathbb{Z}^{6}
, the character group of
T_{1}
.
Denote the generic point of
T_{1}
by
\mathbf{u}=(u_{0},u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4},u_{5})
.
We are going to compute

\displaystyle H\big(S(G(2,6)\big);\mathbf{u}):=\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{15}(-1)^{i}\mathrm{Tor}_{i}^{R_{G(2,6)}}(S_{G(2,6)},\Bbbk)}{\prod_{0\leq i\neq j\leq 5}(1-u_{i}u_{j})}.

as a virtual representation of
T_{1}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{15}(-1)^{i}\mathrm{Tor}_{i}^{R_{G(2,6)}}(S_{G(2,6)},\Bbbk)

(61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=4\end{subarray}}\prod_{i\in S}u_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=5\end{subarray}}\big(\prod_{i\in S}u_{i}\sum_{i\in S}u_{i}\big)+5\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}-\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}\cdot\sum_{0\leq i\leq j\leq 5}u_{i}u_{j}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=5\end{subarray}}\prod_{i\in S}u_{i}^{2}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=4\end{subarray}}\big(\prod_{i\in S}u_{i}^{2}\cdot\prod_{j\not\in S}u_{j}\big)+\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=5\end{subarray}}\big(\prod_{i\in S}u_{i}\sum_{i\in S}u_{i}\big)+5\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle-\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}^{2}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=2\end{subarray}}\big(\prod_{\{i,j\}=S}u_{i}u_{j}\big)+\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}^{3}.

Proof.

We use an explicit resolution of the Plücker ideal of
G(m,n)
, which is known for
m=2
and arbitrary
n
. Let
E
be a
\Bbbk
-vector space with a basis
{v_{0},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4},v_{5}}
, equipped with a
T_{1}
-action with weights
\mathbf{w}(v_{i})=\epsilon_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{6}
,
0\leq i\leq 5
. We identify the ring
R_{G(2,6)}
with the symmetric algebra
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}(\bigwedge^{2}E)
, so that the induced torus action coincides with (
60
). For a 2-dimensional partition
\lambda
let
L_{\lambda}E
be the associated Schur module of
E
(
[
Wey03
, §2.1]
). Each
L_{\lambda}E
is an irreducible
\mathrm{GL}(E)
-representation, and has highest weight
\lambda^{\prime}
(the conjugate partition of
\lambda
).

By
[
Wey03
, Theorem 6.4.1]
, the minimal resolution
P_{\bullet}
of

, as an

-module, has the form

, where

(62)

The character of
L_{\lambda}E
is equal to the Schur function
s_{\lambda^{\prime}}(u_{0},..,u_{5})
. For example,

\displaystyle\mathrm{char}(L_{\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt}(u_{0},..,u_{5})=\det\begin{pmatrix}e_{5}&e_{6}\\
e_{4}&e_{5}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=5\end{subarray}}\prod_{i\in S}u_{i}^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=4\end{subarray}}\big(\prod_{i\in S}u_{i}^{2}\cdot\prod_{j\not\in S}u_{j}\big),

where
e_{i}=e_{i}(u_{0},..,u_{5})
are the elementary symmetric functions.
The characters of the other Schur modules in (
5.1
) can be computed directly from the definition:

\displaystyle\mathrm{char}(L_{\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt}E)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\
|S|=4\end{subarray}}\prod_{i\in S}u_{i},

\displaystyle\mathrm{char}(L_{\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt}E)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\

\displaystyle\mathrm{char}(L_{\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt}=\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}\cdot\sum_{0\leq i\leq j\leq 5}u_{i}u_{j},

\displaystyle\mathrm{char}(L_{\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt}E)=\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\

\displaystyle\mathrm{char}(L_{\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt}E)=\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}^{2}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subset\{0,..5\}\\

\displaystyle\mathrm{char}(L_{\hskip 0.0pt{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt}E)=\prod_{i=0}^{5}u_{i}^{3}.

Summing and expanding, we are done.
∎

Denote the function (

) by

.

.

\displaystyle H(A_{{\lambda}_{121}};\mathbf{t})=K\left(-\frac{\sqrt{t_{2}}\sqrt{t_{3}}}{\sqrt{t_{1}}},-\frac{\sqrt{t_{1}}\sqrt{t_{3}}}{\sqrt{t_{2}}},-\frac{\sqrt{t_{1}}\sqrt{t_{2}}}{\sqrt{t_{3}}},-\frac{t_{2}^{3/2}}{\sqrt{t_{1}}\sqrt{t_{3}}},-\frac{t_{3}^{3/2}}{\sqrt{t_{1}}\sqrt{t_{2}}},-\frac{t_{1}^{3/2}}{\sqrt{t_{2}}\sqrt{t_{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle\Big/\Big((1-t_{1})^{3}(1-t_{2})^{3}(1-t_{3})^{3}(\frac{t_{1}-t_{2}^{2}}{t_{1}})(\frac{t_{1}-t_{2}t_{3}}{t_{1}})(\frac{t_{1}-t_{3}^{2}}{t_{1}})(\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}^{2}}{t_{2}})(\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}t_{3}}{t_{2}})

\displaystyle(\frac{t_{2}-t_{3}^{2}}{t_{2}})(\frac{t_{3}-t_{1}^{2}}{t_{3}})(\frac{t_{3}-t_{1}t_{2}}{t_{3}})(\frac{t_{3}-t_{2}^{2}}{t_{3}})\Big),

\displaystyle H(A_{{\lambda}_{1311}};\mathbf{t})=K\left(-\frac{t_{2}\sqrt{t_{3}}}{t_{1}},-\frac{t_{1}\sqrt{t_{3}}}{t_{2}},-\frac{t_{1}t_{2}}{\sqrt{t_{3}}},-\frac{t_{2}^{2}}{t_{1}\sqrt{t_{3}}},-\frac{t_{3}^{3/2}}{t_{1}t_{2}},-\frac{t_{1}^{2}}{t_{2}\sqrt{t_{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle\Big/\Big((1-t_{1})^{3}(1-t_{2})^{3}(1-t_{3})^{3}(1-t_{1}^{2})(1-t_{2}^{2})(\frac{t_{1}-t_{2}}{t_{1}})(\frac{t_{1}-t_{3}}{t_{1}})(\frac{t_{1}^{2}-t_{2}t_{3}}{t_{1}^{2}})

\displaystyle(\frac{t_{1}^{2}-t_{2}^{3}}{t_{1}^{2}})(\frac{t_{1}^{2}-t_{3}^{2}}{t_{1}^{2}})(\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{t_{2}})(\frac{t_{2}^{2}-t_{1}t_{3}}{t_{2}^{2}})(\frac{t_{2}^{2}-t_{1}^{3}}{t_{2}^{2}})(\frac{t_{2}-t_{3}}{t_{2}})(\frac{t_{2}^{2}-t_{3}^{2}}{t_{2}^{2}})

\displaystyle(\frac{t_{3}-t_{1}t_{2}}{t_{3}})(\frac{t_{3}-t_{1}^{3}}{t_{3}})(\frac{t_{3}-t_{2}^{3}}{t_{3}})\Big).

(66)

Proof.

We only show (
5.2
); the others are similar. The
T=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{3}
action on the Haiman coordinates transfers via (
40
) and (
4.3.1
) to an action on the Plücker coordinates. One checks that this action coincides with the one induced by the map

\displaystyle u_{0}\mapsto-\frac{\sqrt{t_{2}}\sqrt{t_{3}}}{t_{1}},\ u_{1}\mapsto-\frac{t_{1}\sqrt{t_{3}}}{\sqrt{t_{2}}},\ u_{2}\mapsto-\frac{t_{1}\sqrt{t_{2}}}{\sqrt{t_{3}}},

\displaystyle\ u_{3}\mapsto-\frac{t_{2}^{3/2}}{t_{1}\sqrt{t_{3}}},\ u_{4}\mapsto-\frac{t_{3}^{3/2}}{t_{1}\sqrt{t_{2}}},\ u_{5}\mapsto-\frac{t_{1}^{2}}{\sqrt{t_{2}}\sqrt{t_{3}}}.

The appearance of square roots arises from that our choice of the
T_{1}=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{6}
action on
G(2,6)
is not the primitive one. This gives the numerator of (
5.2
). The denominator is computed by the weights of the tangent spaces, via e.g. the description of (
4.14
).
∎

In the same way, in Appendix
A
and
C
we compute the other equivariant Hilbert functions that we need later.

The equivariant Hilbert function of the local ring at
((1)\subset(3,2,1))

2.13
and Lemma
2.14
. We record the result in the following.

.

\displaystyle H(A_{\lambda_{1321}};\mathbf{t})

\displaystyle+t_{2}t_{3}^{7}t_{1}^{3}+t_{3}^{7}t_{1}^{3}-3t_{2}^{5}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{3}-3t_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{3}+t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{3}+2t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{3}+t_{2}^{8}t_{3}^{5}t_{1}^{3}-2t_{2}^{6}t_{3}^{5}t_{1}^{3}+2t_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{5}t_{1}^{3}-3t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{5}t_{1}^{3}

\displaystyle-t_{2}^{8}t_{3}^{2}t_{1}^{3}-2t_{2}^{7}t_{3}^{2}t_{1}^{3}-t_{2}^{5}t_{3}^{2}t_{1}^{3}-t_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{2}t_{1}^{3}-t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{2}t_{1}^{3}-t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{8}t_{1}^{2}-t_{2}t_{3}^{8}t_{1}^{2}+t_{2}^{5}t_{3}^{7}t_{1}^{2}+t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{7}t_{1}^{2}+t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{7}t_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle+t_{2}t_{3}^{7}t_{1}^{2}-2t_{2}^{6}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{2}-2t_{2}^{5}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{2}+t_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{2}+2t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{2}-t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{6}t_{1}^{2}-t_{2}^{7}t_{3}^{5}t_{1}^{2}+t_{2}^{5}t_{3}^{5}t_{1}^{2}-3t_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{5}t_{1}^{2}-3t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{5}t_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\Big/

\displaystyle\Big((t_{1}-1)^{3}\left(t_{1}^{2}-t_{2}\right)^{2}(t_{2}-1)^{3}\left(t_{1}-t_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\left(t_{1}^{3}-t_{2}^{2}\right)\left(t_{1}^{2}-t_{2}^{3}\right)(t_{1}-t_{3})^{3}\left(t_{1}^{3}-t_{3}\right)(t_{2}-t_{3})^{3}

\displaystyle\left(t_{1}^{2}t_{2}-t_{3}\right)\left(t_{1}t_{2}^{2}-t_{3}\right)\left(t_{2}^{3}-t_{3}\right)(t_{3}-1)^{2}(t_{1}+t_{3})(t_{2}+t_{3})\left(t_{1}t_{3}-t_{2}^{2}\right)\left(t_{1}^{2}-t_{2}t_{3}\right)\left(t_{1}t_{2}-t_{3}^{2}\right)\Big).

5.3
The localization computation

Conjecture 5.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective scheme. For line bundles
K,L
on
X
,

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\chi(\Lambda_{-v}K^{[n]},\Lambda_{-u}L^{[n]})Q^{n}=\exp\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\chi(\Lambda_{-v^{n}}K,\Lambda_{-u^{n}}L)\frac{Q^{n}}{n}\Big).

(67)

In particular,

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\chi(L^{[n]})Q^{n}=(1-Q)^{-\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X})}\chi(L)Q.

Conjecture 5.5
.

Denote by
\mathscr{P}_{r}
the set of
r
-dimensional partitions (an empty partition is allowed). For
\lambda\in\mathscr{P}_{r}
, recall that
A_{\lambda}
is the coordinate ring of the Haiman neighborhood, and
H(A_{\lambda};\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{r})
is the equivariant Hilbert function of
A_{\lambda}
.
Then for
r\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda\in\mathscr{P}_{r}}\Big(Q^{|\lambda|}H(A_{\lambda};\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{r})\prod_{\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{r})\in\lambda}(1-u\theta_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots\theta_{r}^{i_{r}})(1-v\theta_{1}^{-i_{1}}\cdots\theta_{r}^{-i_{r}})\Big)

(68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(1-u^{n})(1-v^{n})Q^{n}}{n(1-\theta_{1}^{n})\cdots(1-\theta_{r}^{n})}\right).

Remark 5.6
.

Conjecture
5.5
is a refinement of
[
WZ14
, Conjecture 3]
. Note that when
r=1
, (
68
) is not true, but it is still true if
v=0
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
X
be a smooth proper toric variety over
\Bbbk
of dimension
r
. Let
T=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r}
be the open dense torus contained in
X
. Let
K
,
L
be two
T
-line bundles on
X
. Then Conjecture
5.5
implies Conjecture
5.4
for
X
,
K
and
L
. More precisely, if Conjecture
5.5
holds modulo
Q^{s}
for some
s>0
, Conjecture
5.4
modulo
Q^{s}
also holds for such triples
(X,K,L)
.

Proof.

The
T
-action on
X
has only isolated fixed points, denoted by
x_{1},\dots,x_{m}
. For
1\leq b\leq m
, let
\mathbf{w}_{b,1},\dots,\mathbf{w}_{b,r}
be the weights of the cotangent space of
X
at
x_{b}
. Let
\mathbf{k}_{b}
(resp.
\mathbf{l}_{b}
) be the weight of
K
(resp.
L
) at
x_{b}
. For
n\geq 1
, let
\alpha_{n}:T\rightarrow T
be the homomorphism
\mathbf{t}=(t_{1},\dots,t_{r})\mapsto\mathbf{t}^{n}=(t_{1}^{n},\dots,t_{r}^{n})
. Recall our notations introduced before Example
2.11
. For each
n\geq 1
, applying (
9
) to
X
, with the action on
X
,
K
and
L
by
T
precomposed with
\alpha_{n}
, we obtain

\chi(\Lambda_{-v^{n}}K,\Lambda_{-u^{n}}L)=\sum_{b=1}^{m}\frac{(1-u^{n}\mathbf{t}^{-n\mathbf{k_{b}}})(1-v^{n}\mathbf{t}^{n\mathbf{l}_{b}})}{\prod_{j=1}^{r}(1-\mathbf{t}^{nw_{b,j}})}.

(69)

Summing over
n
we get an equality of series of virtual
T
-representations

\exp\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\chi(\Lambda_{-v^{n}}K,\Lambda_{-u^{n}}L)\frac{Q^{n}}{n}\Big)=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\exp\Big(\frac{(1-u^{n}\mathbf{t}^{-n\mathbf{k_{i}}})(1-v^{n}\mathbf{t}^{n\mathbf{l}_{i}})}{n\prod_{j=1}^{r}(1-\mathbf{t}^{nw_{i,j}})}Q^{n}\Big).

(70)

Now assume Conjecture
5.5
valid. Replacing
u
in (
68
) by
u\mathbf{t}^{-\mathbf{k}_{b}}
,
v
by
v\mathbf{t}^{\mathbf{l}_{b}}
, and
\theta_{j}
by
\mathbf{t}^{\mathbf{w}_{b,j}}
for
1\leq j\leq r
, we have

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\frac{(1-u^{n}\mathbf{t}^{-n\mathbf{k_{i}}})(1-v^{n}\mathbf{t}^{n\mathbf{l}_{i}})}{n\prod_{j=1}^{r}(1-\mathbf{t}^{nw_{b,j}})}Q^{n}\Big)

(71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda\in\mathscr{P}_{r}}\Big(Q^{|\lambda|}H(A_{\lambda};\mathbf{t}^{w_{b,1}},\dots,\mathbf{t}^{w_{b,r}})\prod_{\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{r})\in\lambda}(1-u\mathbf{t}^{-\mathbf{k}_{b}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbf{t}^{i_{j}\cdot w_{b,j}})\Big).

Consider the
T
-action on
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(X)
induced by the action on
X
(not involving
\alpha_{n}
). The equivariant local structure of
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(X)
at a fixed subscheme
Z
of length
l
supported at
x_{b}
is isomorphic to the equivariant local structure of
\mathrm{Hilb}^{l}(\mathbb{A}^{r})
at
Z_{I}
supported at
0
, where
I
is a monomial ideal, and where the
T
-action on
\mathbb{A}
by weights
\mathbf{w}_{b,1},\dots,\mathbf{w}_{b,r}
(see the proof of Proposition
3.3
). By (
9
) or (
11
) we obtain an equality in
R(T)

\displaystyle\chi(\Lambda_{-v}K^{[n]},\Lambda_{-u}L^{[n]})

(72)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{m}\in\mathscr{P}_{r}\\
|\lambda_{1}|+\dots+|\lambda_{m}|=r\end{subarray}}\sum_{b=1}^{m}\Big(H(A_{\lambda};\mathbf{t}^{w_{b,1}},\dots,\mathbf{t}^{w_{b,r}})\prod_{\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{r})\in\lambda_{b}}(1-u\mathbf{t}^{-\mathbf{k}_{b}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbf{t}^{i_{j}\cdot w_{b,j}})\Big).

Summing over
n\geq 0
, and combining (
70
), (
71
) and (
72
), we obtain the validity of (
67
) for the equivariant triple
(X,K,L)
, as an equality of series of virtual representations of
T
. Taking the limit
\mathbf{t}\rightarrow 1
we complete the proof of the first statement. The second statement follows by ignoring the higher order terms of
Q
in the above proof.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Conjecture
5.5
modulo
Q^{7}
holds for smooth proper toric 3-folds
X
and equivariant line bundles
K,L
on
X
. Assume that Conjecture
4.23
is true, then Conjecture
5.5
modulo
Q^{8}
holds for smooth proper toric 3-folds
X
and equivariant line bundles
K,L
on
X
.

Proof.

The computations of equivariant Hilbert functions
H(A_{\lambda};\mathbf{t})
at singular points are done in Corollary
5.2
and Proposition
5.3
, and appendices
A
and
C
. The contribution of smooth points is computed using the description of the cotangent spaces in Lemma
4.14
, and its implementation in Macaulay2. Then we verify (
68
) by brute force.

∎

Remark 5.9
.

The right handside of (
68
) is manifestly symmetric in
u
and
v
, while the left handside seems not. Replacing
v
by
v^{-1}
, and
Q
by
vQ
, we obtains a formula equivalent to (
68
), which is symmetric in
u
and
v
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda}\Big((-1)^{|\lambda|}Q^{|\lambda|}H(A_{\lambda};\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{r})\prod_{i\in\lambda}\theta^{-i}\cdot\prod_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{r})\in\lambda}(1-u\theta_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots\theta_{r}^{i_{r}})(1-v\theta_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots\theta_{r}^{i_{r}})\Big)

(73)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(1-u^{n})(1-v^{n})Q^{n}}{n(1-\theta_{1}^{n})\cdots(1-\theta_{r}^{n})}\right).

Remark 5.10
.

It is very desirable to have, at least a conjectural, formula for every single
H(A_{\lambda};\mathbf{t})
. Then it is probable to show (
68
), or (
73
), as a combinatorial identity, and thus avoid the cumbersome verifications in the final step of the proof of Proposition
5.8
, once and for all.

Remark 5.11
.

The equivariant Hilbert functions
H(A_{\lambda};\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{r})
in Corollary
5.2
and Proposition
5.3
satisfy the
self reciprocal law

H(A_{\lambda};\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{r})=(-1)^{|\lambda|}(\theta_{1}\cdots\theta_{r})^{-|\lambda|}\prod_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{r})\in\lambda}\theta_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots\theta_{r}^{i_{r}}\cdot H(A_{\lambda};\theta_{1}^{-1},\dots,\theta_{r}^{-1}).

(74)

This is related to the Gorenstein property by Theorem
2.15
.

By Proposition
5.7
and
5.8
we obtain:

Corollary 5.12
.

Conjecture
5.4
modulo
Q^{7}
holds for smooth proper toric 3-folds
X
and
T
-line bundles
K,L
on
X
, where
T
is the dense open torus in
X
.
Assume that Conjecture
4.23
is true, then Conjecture
5.4
modulo
Q^{8}
holds for smooth proper toric 3-folds
X
and equivariant line bundles
K,L
on
X
.

Remark 5.13
.

In
[
Hu21
]
, we show that Conjecture
5.4
can be reduced to the cases that
X
is a product of projective spaces, and
K
,
L
are exterior tensor products of the line bundles of the form
\mathcal{O}(k)
. Thus it follows that Conjecture
5.4
modulo
Q^{8}
holds for all smooth proper 3-fold.

5.4
A McKay correspondence

Denote the symmetric group of cardinality
n!
by
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
.
Denote by
X^{(n)}=X^{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}
the
n
-th symmetric product of
X
. There is the Hilbert-Chow morphism
\rho:X^{[n]}\rightarrow X^{(n)}
(see
[
Ber12
, §2.2]
); set theoretically it sends a
0
-dimensional subscheme to the associated 0-cycle.

Conjecture 5.14
.

(K-theoretical pushforward)

\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{[n]}}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{(n)}}
in
K_{0}(X^{(n)})
.

For a smooth projective surface, this conjecture follows from
[
Sca09
, Prop. 1.3.2, 1.3.3]
.

Let
L
be a line bundle on
X
. Denote by
p_{i}:X^{n}\rightarrow X
the
i
-th projection. Let
L_{i}=p_{i}^{*}L
. Consider the quotient stack
[X/\mathfrak{S}_{n}]
. There is an obvious action of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
on the vector bundle
\oplus_{i=1}^{n}L_{i}
, rendering
\oplus_{i=1}^{n}L_{i}\rightarrow X^{n}
equivariant, which gives a vector bundle

[\oplus_{i=1}^{n}L_{i}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}]
on
[X^{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}]
. Denote by
\pi:[X/\mathfrak{S}_{n}]\rightarrow X^{(n)}
the projection to the coarse moduli space. Thus
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{[X^{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}]}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{(n)}}
.

Conjecture 5.15
.

Suppose
\dim X\geq 2
.
Let
K,L
be line bundles on
X
. For the vector bundles
U=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}^{*}K
,
V=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}^{*}L
on
X^{n}

the K-theoretical pushforward

\rho_{*}\Big(\Lambda_{-u}\big((K^{[n]})^{*}\big)\otimes\Lambda_{-v}\big(L^{[n]}\big)\Big)\cong\pi_{*}[(\Lambda_{-u}U^{*}\otimes\Lambda_{-v}V)/\mathfrak{S}_{n}].

For
u=0
and
\dim X=2
, this conjecture follows from
[
Sca09
, prop. 2.4.5]
. We regard this conjecture as a sort of McKay correspondence:

\textstyle{[X^{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X^{[n]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{X^{(n)}}

Although
X^{[n]}
is not smooth in general, it is a modular substitute of the quotient scheme
X^{(n)}
.
In the following of this subsection we show that Conjecture
5.15
implies Conjecture
5.4
. The tool is the Riemann-Roch theorem for smooth Deligne-Mumford quotient stacks (
[
EG05
]
).
Let us recall some notations. We follow the presentation of
[
Edi13
]
.

Let
Y
be a smooth projective scheme over
\Bbbk
of characteristic zero, with an action by a finite group
G
. Let
\Psi_{1},\dots,\Psi_{m}
be the conjugacy classes of
G
. Choose a representative
g_{k}\in\Psi_{i}
for each
1\leq k\leq m
. For any element
g\in G
, let
Z_{g}
be the centralizer of
g
in
G
, and let
H_{g}
be the subgroup generated by
g
. Let
Y^{g_{k}}
be the fixed subscheme of
Y
, and
\iota_{k}:Y^{g_{k}}\hookrightarrow Y
be the closed immersion. Since the subgroup
H_{g_{k}}
is diagonalizable,
Y^{g_{k}}
is regular. Let
N_{\iota_{k}}
be the normal bundle of
Y^{g_{k}}
in
Y
.

Let
E
be a
G
-equivariant vector bundle on
Y
. Now assume that
\Bbbk
is algebraically closed. The restriction of
E
on
Y^{g_{k}}
, decomposes into a sum of
g_{k}
-eigenbundles
\bigoplus_{\xi\in\mathbb{X}(H_{g_{k}})}E_{\xi}
, where
\mathbb{X}(H)
is the group of characters of an abelian group
H
. Define

t_{h_{k}}(E)=\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{X}(H_{g_{k}})}\xi(g_{k})E_{\xi}.

(75)

Here
t
means
twisting
, and does not indicate any relation to a torus.
Then the Riemann-Roch theorem for the Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{Y}=[Y/G]
says

\chi(\mathcal{Y},E)=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\int_{[Y^{g_{k}}/Z_{g_{k}}]}\mathrm{ch}\Big(t_{g_{k}}\big(\frac{{\iota_{k}}^{*}E}{\lambda_{-1}(N^{*}_{\iota_{k}})}\big)\Big)\mathrm{Td}([Y^{g_{k}}/Z_{g_{k}}]).

(76)

Remark 5.16
.

In
[
EG05
]
the base field is assumed to be
\mathbb{C}
, and the group
G
is a general algebraic group. In our case,
G
is a finite abstract group, so the formula is simpler. And in this case the assumption that the base field is algebraically closed is used only in the decomposition into eigenbundles. The assumption on characteristic zero is essential, for the sufficiently higher cohomology to vanish so that the left handside of (
76
) to be defined.

For a vector bundle
F
, by the splitting principle, we assume

F=\oplus_{i=1}^{r}W_{i}
, where
W_{i}
are line bundles, and define

\Upsilon_{m}(F)=\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{e^{-mc_{1}(W_{i})}-1}{e^{-c_{1}(W_{i})}-1}.

Lemma 5.17
.

(i)

Let
\zeta_{m}=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{m}}
. Then

\Upsilon_{m}(F)=\prod_{k=1}^{m-1}\sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}\zeta_{m}^{k}\mathrm{ch}(\Lambda^{i}(F^{*})).

(77)

(ii)

Let
T_{X}
be the tangent bundle of
X
. Then

\int_{X}\frac{\mathrm{ch}(L)^{m}\mathrm{Td}(X)}{\Upsilon_{m}(T_{X})}=\int_{X}\frac{e^{mc_{1}(L)}\mathrm{Td}(X)}{\Upsilon_{m}(T_{X})}=\chi(L),

(78)

\int_{X}\frac{\mathrm{ch}(K^{*})^{m}\mathrm{ch}(L)^{m}\mathrm{Td}(X)}{\Upsilon_{m}(T_{X})}=\int_{X}\frac{e^{-mc_{1}(K)}e^{mc_{1}(L)}\mathrm{Td}(X)}{\Upsilon_{m}(T_{X})}=\chi(K^{*}\otimes L).

(79)

Proof.

(i)

\prod_{k=1}^{m-1}\sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}\zeta_{m}^{k}\mathrm{ch}(\Lambda^{i}(F^{*}))=\prod_{k=1}^{m-1}\mathrm{ch}\big((1-\zeta_{m}^{k}W_{1}^{*})\cdots(1-\zeta_{m}^{k}W_{r}^{*})\big)\\
=\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\mathrm{ch}(W_{i}^{*})^{m}-1}{\mathrm{ch}(W_{i}^{*})-1}=\prod_{i=1}^{r}\frac{e^{-mc_{1}(W_{i})}-1}{e^{-c_{1}(W_{i})}-1}.

(ii)
Let
x_{1},\dots,x_{\dim X}
be the Chern roots of
TX
. Then

\int_{X}\frac{\mathrm{ch}(L)^{m}\mathrm{Td}(X)}{\Upsilon_{m}(TX)}=\int_{X}e^{mc_{1}(L)}\prod_{i=1}^{\dim X}\frac{x_{i}}{1-e^{-mx_{i}}}=\frac{1}{m^{\dim X}}\int_{X}e^{mc_{1}(L)}\prod_{i=1}^{\dim X}\frac{mx_{i}}{1-e^{-mx_{i}}}\\
=\frac{1}{m^{\dim X}}\cdot m^{\dim X}\int_{X}e^{c_{1}(L)}\prod_{i=1}^{\dim X}\frac{x_{i}}{1-e^{-x_{i}}}=\int_{X}\mathrm{ch}(L)\mathrm{Td}(X)=\chi(L).

The proof of (
79
) is similar.
∎

For a (usual) partition
\mu
, let
z_{\mu}=\prod_{i\geq 1}i^{k_{i}}k_{i}!
, where
k_{i}
is the number of parts of
\mu
equal to
i
. The following identity is obtained by directly expanding the right handside.

Lemma 5.18
.

For indeterminates
Y_{1},Y_{2},\dots
we have the identity

1+\sum_{\mu=(m_{1},\dots,m_{l})}\frac{1}{z_{\mu}}\big(\prod_{j=1}^{l}Y_{m_{j}}Q^{|\mu|}\big)=\exp\big(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}Y_{r}\frac{Q^{r}}{r}\big),

(80)

where the sum on the left handside runs over all partitions of natural numbers.

Theorem 5.19
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective scheme over a field of characteristic zero. For line bundles
K,L
on
X
, let
U=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}^{*}K
, and
V=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}^{*}L
. Then

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\chi(X^{(n)},\pi_{*}[(\Lambda_{-u}U^{*}\otimes\Lambda_{-v}V)/\mathfrak{S}_{n}])Q^{n}=\exp\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\chi(\Lambda_{-v^{n}}K,\Lambda_{-u^{n}}L)\frac{Q^{n}}{n}\Big).

(81)

Proof.

The formation of the quotient
X^{(n)}
commutes with flat base changes, so we can assume that
\Bbbk
is algebraically closed.
The conjugacy classes of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
correspond to the partitions of
n
.
Let
\mu=(m_{1},\dots,m_{l})
be a partition of
n
, and we use also
\mu
to represent the conjugacy class corresponding to
\mu
. Let
h_{\mu}
be the element of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
which preserves the
l
parts of
\mu
, and in the
i
-th part is the addition by 1 mod
m_{i}
. Formally,
h_{\mu}
is uniquely determined by the following requirement: for
1\leq k\leq l
, and
1\leq i\leq m_{k}
,

\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}m_{j}<h_{\mu}(i+\sum_{j=1}^{k}m_{j})\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}m_{j}

and

h_{\mu}(i+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}m_{j})\equiv i+1+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}m_{j}\mod(m_{k}).

The fixed locus of
h_{\mu}
is
(X^{n})^{h_{\mu}}=(\Delta_{X})_{1}\times\cdots\times(\Delta_{X})_{l}
, where
(\Delta_{X})_{k}\cong X
is the small diagonal in
X^{m_{k}}
.
Denote by
\iota_{\mu}:(X^{n})^{h_{\mu}}\hookrightarrow X^{n}

the obvious closed immersion. For
1\leq j\leq l
denote by

q_{j}:(X^{n})^{h_{\mu}}=(\Delta_{X})_{1}\times\cdots\times(\Delta_{X})_{l}\rightarrow(\Delta_{X})_{j}

the
j
-th projection.
The order of
h_{\mu}
is
M_{\mu}=\mathrm{gcd}(m_{1},\dots,m_{l})
.
The center of
h_{\mu}
is denoted by
Z_{\mu}
, and its order is
z_{\mu}
.
The twisting operator (
75
) according to
h_{\mu}
is denoted by
t_{\mu}
.
We have

i_{\mu}^{*}V=\oplus_{j=1}^{l}q_{j}^{*}(L^{\oplus m_{j}})

and

t_{\mu}(i_{\mu}^{*}V)=\sum_{j=1}^{l}p_{j}^{*}(\sum_{k=0}^{m_{j}-1}e^{\frac{2k\pi\sqrt{-1}}{m_{j}}}L),

and

t_{\mu}(\lambda_{-1}(N_{X^{\mu}}^{*}))=\sum_{j=1}^{l}p_{j}^{*}\big(\prod_{k=1}^{m_{j}-1}(\sum_{i=0}^{\dim X}(-1)^{i}e^{\frac{2ki\pi\sqrt{-1}}{m_{j}}}\Lambda^{i}T^{*}_{X})\big).

So by (
77
) we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{ch}\Big(t_{\mu}\big(\frac{i_{\mu}^{*}(\Lambda_{-u}U^{*}\otimes\Lambda_{-v}V)}{\lambda_{-1}(N_{X^{\mu}}^{*})}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{l}p_{j}^{*}\prod_{k=0}^{m_{j}-1}\big(1-ue^{\frac{2k\pi\sqrt{-1}}{m_{j}}}\mathrm{ch}(K^{*})\big)\big(1-ve^{\frac{2k\pi\sqrt{-1}}{m_{j}}}\mathrm{ch}(L)\big)}{\prod_{j=1}^{l}p_{j}^{*}\Upsilon_{m_{j}}(T_{X})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{l}p_{j}^{*}\big(1-u^{m_{j}}\mathrm{ch}(K^{*})^{m}\big)\big(1-v^{m_{j}}\mathrm{ch}(L)^{m}\big)}{\prod_{j=1}^{l}p_{j}^{*}\Upsilon_{m_{j}}(T_{X})},

then using (
78
) and (
79
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{[X^{\mu}/Z_{\mu}]}\mathrm{ch}\Big(t_{\mu}\big(\frac{i_{\mu}^{*}(\Lambda_{-u}U^{*}\otimes\Lambda_{-v}V)}{\lambda_{-1}(N_{X^{\mu}}^{*})}\big)\Big)\mathrm{Td}([X^{\mu}/Z_{\mu}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z_{\mu}}\prod_{j=1}^{l}\big(\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X})-u^{m_{j}}\chi(K^{*})-v^{m_{j}}\chi(L)+u^{m_{j}}v^{m_{j}}\chi(K^{*}\otimes L)\big).

In (
80
) we set

Y_{m}=\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X})-u^{m}\chi(K^{*})-v^{m}\chi(L)+u^{m}v^{m}\chi(K^{*}\otimes L).

Then we obtain

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\chi\big([(\Lambda_{-u}U^{*}\otimes\Lambda_{-v}V)/\mathfrak{S}_{n}]\big)Q^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{1}{z_{\mu}}\prod_{j=1}^{l}\big(\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X})-u^{m}\chi(K^{*})-v^{m}\chi(L)+u^{m}v^{m}\chi(K^{*}\otimes L)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\big(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}(\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X})-u^{r}\chi(K^{*})-v^{r}\chi(L)+u^{r}v^{r}\chi(K^{*}\otimes L))\frac{Q^{r}}{r}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\chi(\Lambda_{-u^{r}}K,\Lambda_{-v^{r}}L)\frac{Q^{r}}{r}\Big).

∎

6  Local properties of Hilbert schemes

In this section we study certain properties of the singularities encountered in this paper.

Proposition 6.1
.

\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{121}})
is normal, Gorenstein, and has only rational singularities.

Proof.

The first two properties follows from the general fact that Grassmannians with respect to Plücker embeddings are arithmetically normal and arithmetically Gorenstein (e.g.
[
LB15
, §6.3 & §7.5]
).
More explicitly, we have a regular sequence
a,c,d,e,g,h,f+m+q,i+p+r,j+l+n
for the ring (see
43
)

A=\Bbbk[a,c,d,e,f,g,h,i,j,l,m,n,p,q,r]/J_{121}.

(82)

The annihilator of the maximal ideal
P=(a,c,d,e,f,g,h,i,j,l,m,n,p,q,r)
in

\Bbbk[a,c,d,e,f,g,h,i,j,l,m,n,p,q,r]/\big(J_{121}+(a,c,d,e,g,h,f+m+q,i+p+r,j+l+n)\big)

is a principal ideal generated by
r^{3}
. So by definition, (
82
) is Gorenstein. By Serre’s criterion, Cohen-Macaulay plus regularity in codimension 1 implies normal. So
A_{\lambda_{121}}
is normal for it has an isolated singularity at
P
.

For the third property, blowing up the cone (
82
) at the origin, the exceptional divisor
E
is isomorphic to
G(2,6)
, with normal sheaf
\mathcal{O}(-1)
. Denote the total space of the normal bundle by
N
. We need only to show
H^{i}(N,\mathcal{O}_{N})=0
for
i>0
. Since
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{N}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{N}^{\vee})
, where
\pi:N\rightarrow E
is the projection, we are left to show

H^{i}(G(2,6),\mathcal{O}(j))=0

for
i,j>0
. This follows from the Kodaira vanishing, for the canonical bundle
K_{G(m,n)}\cong\mathcal{O}(-n)
.

∎

In the rest of this section we study
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{1321}})
. Look at the superpotential

\displaystyle F_{1321}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{y}_{5}{y}_{8}{y}_{10}{y}_{14}+{y}_{6}{y}_{7}{y}_{14}{y}_{15}+{y}_{8}{y}_{9}{y}_{10}{y}_{17}-{y}_{2}{y}_{7}{y}_{15}{y}_{17}+{y}_{4}{y}_{10}{y}_{15}{y}_{17}-{y}_{6}{y}_{8}{y}_{14}{y}_{18}

\displaystyle+{y}_{2}{y}_{8}{y}_{17}{y}_{18}-{y}_{10}{y}_{14}{y}_{15}{y}_{19}+{y}_{2}{y}_{5}{y}_{7}{y}_{21}-{y}_{6}{y}_{7}{y}_{9}{y}_{21}-{y}_{4}{y}_{5}{y}_{10}{y}_{21}-{y}_{4}{y}_{6}{y}_{18}{y}_{21}

\displaystyle+{y}_{9}{y}_{10}{y}_{19}{y}_{21}+{y}_{2}{y}_{18}{y}_{19}{y}_{21}+{y}_{2}{y}_{5}{y}_{8}{y}_{24}-{y}_{6}{y}_{8}{y}_{9}{y}_{24}-{y}_{4}{y}_{6}{y}_{15}{y}_{24}+{y}_{2}{y}_{15}{y}_{19}{y}_{24}

\displaystyle-{y}_{5}{y}_{7}{y}_{14}{y}_{27}+{y}_{7}{y}_{9}{y}_{17}{y}_{27}+{y}_{4}{y}_{17}{y}_{18}{y}_{27}-{y}_{14}{y}_{18}{y}_{19}{y}_{27}+{y}_{4}{y}_{5}{y}_{24}{y}_{27}-{y}_{9}{y}_{19}{y}_{24}{y}_{27}

\displaystyle-{y}_{11}{y}_{13}{y}_{14}+{y}_{1}{y}_{7}{y}_{16}+{y}_{4}{y}_{11}{y}_{16}+{y}_{8}{y}_{12}{y}_{16}-{y}_{3}{y}_{13}{y}_{17}+{y}_{3}{y}_{16}{y}_{19}-{y}_{3}{y}_{6}{y}_{20}

\displaystyle-{y}_{1}{y}_{10}{y}_{20}-{y}_{2}{y}_{11}{y}_{20}+{y}_{12}{y}_{13}{y}_{21}-{y}_{1}{y}_{13}{y}_{24}+{y}_{12}{y}_{20}{y}_{27}-{y}_{3}{y}_{5}{y}_{29}-{y}_{9}{y}_{11}{y}_{29}

\displaystyle+{y}_{12}{y}_{15}{y}_{29}+{y}_{1}{y}_{18}{y}_{29}.

The variables
y_{22},y_{23},y_{25},y_{26},y_{28}
do not appear in the above expression.
It has the
\mathbb{Z}/2
symmetry

\displaystyle F_{1321}({y}_{1},{y}_{2},{y}_{3},{y}_{4},{y}_{5},{y}_{6},{y}_{7},{y}_{8},{y}_{9},{y}_{10},{y}_{11},{y}_{12},{y}_{13},{y}_{14},{y}_{15},

(83)

\displaystyle{y}_{16},{y}_{17},{y}_{18},{y}_{19},{y}_{20},{y}_{21},{y}_{22},{y}_{23},{y}_{24},{y}_{25},{y}_{26},{y}_{27},{y}_{28},{y}_{29})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{1321}({y}_{1},{-{y}_{9}},{y}_{12},{y}_{14},{y}_{27},{y}_{15},{y}_{24},{y}_{17},{-{y}_{2}},{y}_{18},{y}_{11},{y}_{3},{y}_{16},{y}_{4},{y}_{6},

\displaystyle{y}_{13},{y}_{8},{y}_{10},{-{y}_{21}},{y}_{29},{-{y}_{19}},{y}_{22},{y}_{23},{y}_{7},{y}_{25},{y}_{26},{y}_{5},{y}_{28},{y}_{20}).

In this section we set
R=\mathbb{Q}[y_{1},\dots,y_{29}]/\mathrm{Jac}(F_{1321})
, and

S=R/(y_{22},y_{23},y_{25},y_{26},y_{28})
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Away from
0
,
\mathrm{Spec}(S)
has extra dimension equal to 0 or 6, and has at most rational singularities. More precisely, every point away from
0
, is a smooth point, or has an open neighborhood that is isomorphic to an open subset of a trivial affine bundle over the cone
\widehat{G}(2,6)
.

Proof.

We use the notations in Section
4.3.4
. Let
I
be an ideal of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
such that
Z_{I}
lies in the Haiman neighborhood
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{1321}})
. By Theorem
4.9
(iii), this implies that

1,X_{1},X_{1}^{2},X_{2},X_{1}X_{2},X_{2}^{2},X_{3}

(84)

form a basis of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]/I
. Suppose
Z_{I}
lies the open subset
\{x_{4}\neq 0\}
. Recall
x_{4}=c_{2,0,0}^{0,1,1}
. Then

1,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{1}X_{2},X_{2}^{2},X_{2}X_{3}

(85)

form a basis of
\Bbbk[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]/I
. By Theorem
4.9
(iii), this implies that
I
lies in the Haiman neighborhood of the ideal
J=(X_{1}^{2},X_{1}X_{2}^{2},X_{1}X_{3},X_{2}^{3},X_{2}^{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2})
. After the permutation
X_{1}\leftrightarrow X_{2}
,
J
is transformed to the ideal
I_{\lambda_{232}}
in appendix
A.4
. The ideal
I_{\lambda_{232}}
is a Borel tripod ideal with extra dimension 6. By the result of
A.4
and Proposition
6.1
,
\mathrm{Hilb}^{7}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
is smooth or a rational singularity at
Z_{I}
.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\{x_{8}=c_{2,0,0}^{0,3,0}\neq 0\}\subset\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{(X_{1}^{2},X_{1}X_{2}^{2},X_{1}X_{3},X_{2}^{4},X_{2}X_{3},X_{3}^{2})}})\cong\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{142}}),

\displaystyle\{x_{13}=c_{0,0,1}^{0,3,0}\neq 0\}\subset\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{(X_{1}^{3},X_{1}^{2}X_{2},X_{1}X_{2}^{2},X_{2}^{4},X_{3})}}),

(86a)

\displaystyle\{x_{14}=c_{0,2,0}^{1,0,1}\neq 0\}\subset\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{232}}),

\displaystyle\{x_{16}=c_{0,0,1}^{3,0,0}\neq 0\}\subset\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{(X_{1}^{4},X_{1}^{2}X_{2},X_{1}X_{2}^{2},X_{2}^{3},X_{3})}}),

(86b)

\displaystyle\{x_{17}=c_{0,2,0}^{3,0,0}\neq 0\}\subset\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{142}}),

\displaystyle\{x_{20}=c_{0,0,1}^{1,2,0}\neq 0\}\subset\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{(X_{1}^{3},X_{1}^{2}X_{2},X_{2}^{3},X_{3})}}),

(86c)

\displaystyle\{x_{29}=c_{0,0,1}^{2,1,0}\neq 0\}\subset\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\lambda_{(X_{1}^{3},X_{1}X_{2}^{2},X_{2}^{3},X_{3})}}).

(86d)

Thus by the results of Appendix
A.4
and
A.3
, when one of the coordinates
x_{8},x_{14},x_{17}
is not zero, the point is smooth or a rational singularity. By Lemma
4.15
, each of the Haiman neighborhoods (
86a
)-(

) isomorphic to the product of
\mathbb{A}^{7}
with a Haiman neighborhood in
\mathrm{Hilb}^{7}(\mathbb{A}^{2})
.
Thus when one of the coordinates
x_{13},x_{16},x_{20},x_{29}
is not zero, the point is smooth.

Note that on
S
we have
y_{22}=y_{23}=y_{25}=y_{26}=y_{28}=0
, which is equivalent to
x_{22}=x_{23}=x_{25}=x_{26}=x_{28}=0
. It remains to show that if one of the coordinates

x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{5},x_{6},x_{7},x_{9},x_{10},x_{11},x_{12},x_{15},x_{18},x_{19},x_{21},x_{24},x_{27}

is nonzero, then the point is smooth or a rational singularity. By (
56
) we consider the
y
-coordinates. By the
\mathbb{Z}/2
symmetry (
83
) we need only to consider
y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{5},y_{6},y_{7},y_{10},y_{11},y_{19}
.

If
y_{1}\neq 0
, the relations in
\mathrm{Jac}(F_{1321})
implies

\displaystyle y_{24}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{11}y_{14}+y_{12}y_{21}-y_{17}y_{3}}{y_{1}},

\displaystyle y_{7}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{11}y_{4}-y_{12}y_{8}-y_{19}y_{3}}{y_{1}},

\displaystyle y_{10}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{11}y_{2}+y_{12}y_{27}-y_{3}y_{6}}{y_{1}},

\displaystyle y_{18}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{y_{11}y_{9}-y_{12}y_{15}+y_{3}y_{5}}{y_{1}},

\displaystyle y_{16}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{14}y_{15}y_{6}+y_{14}y_{27}y_{5}+y_{15}y_{17}y_{2}-y_{17}y_{27}y_{9}-y_{2}y_{21}y_{5}+y_{21}y_{6}y_{9}}{y_{1}},

\displaystyle y_{20}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{14}y_{15}y_{19}-y_{14}y_{5}y_{8}+y_{15}y_{17}y_{4}+y_{17}y_{8}y_{9}+y_{19}y_{21}y_{9}-y_{21}y_{4}y_{5}}{y_{1}},

\displaystyle y_{29}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{y_{14}y_{19}y_{27}+y_{14}y_{6}y_{8}-y_{17}y_{2}y_{8}-y_{17}y_{27}y_{4}-y_{19}y_{2}y_{21}+y_{21}y_{4}y_{6}}{y_{1}},

\displaystyle y_{13}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{y_{15}y_{19}y_{2}-y_{15}y_{4}y_{6}-y_{19}y_{27}y_{9}+y_{2}y_{5}y_{8}+y_{27}y_{4}y_{5}-y_{6}y_{8}y_{9}}{y_{1}}.

Substituting these equations into
\mathrm{Jac}(F_{1321})
we obtain a zero ideal.
So
\mathrm{Spec}(S)
is an open subset of an affine space near such points. Similarly one can show this on
\{y_{3}\neq 0\}
and
\{y_{11}\neq 0\}
.

If
y_{2}\neq 0
, the relations in
\mathrm{Jac}(F_{1321})
implies

\displaystyle y_{20}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{13}y_{14}+y_{16}y_{4}-y_{29}y_{9}}{y_{2}},

\displaystyle y_{11}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-y_{1}y_{10}+y_{12}y_{27}-y_{3}y_{6}}{y_{2}}.

Substituting these equations into
\mathrm{Jac}(F_{1231})
, and then making the following change of variables

\displaystyle y_{5}\mapsto y_{5}+\frac{y_{6}y_{9}}{y_{2}},

\displaystyle y_{7}\mapsto y_{7}+\frac{y_{10}y_{4}}{y_{2}},

\displaystyle y_{8}\mapsto y_{8}-\frac{y_{27}y_{4}}{y_{2}},

\displaystyle y_{15}\mapsto y_{15}+\frac{y_{27}y_{9}}{y_{2}},

\displaystyle y_{17}\mapsto y_{17}+\frac{y_{14}y_{6}}{y_{2}},

\displaystyle y_{18}\mapsto y_{18}-\frac{y_{10}y_{9}}{y_{2}},

(87)

\displaystyle y_{19}\mapsto y_{19}+\frac{y_{4}y_{6}}{y_{2}},

\displaystyle y_{21}\mapsto y_{21}+\frac{y_{14}y_{27}}{y_{2}},

\displaystyle y_{24}\mapsto y_{24}+\frac{y_{10}y_{14}}{y_{2}},

the ideal
\mathrm{Jac}(F_{1231})
is transformed into the ideal

\displaystyle(-y_{13}y_{24}+y_{16}y_{7}+y_{18}y_{29},

\displaystyle-y_{13}y_{17}+y_{16}y_{19}-y_{29}y_{5},

\displaystyle y_{2}y_{21}y_{7}+y_{2}y_{24}y_{8}-y_{29}y_{3},

\displaystyle y_{1}y_{16}-y_{15}y_{17}y_{2}+y_{2}y_{21}y_{5},

\displaystyle y_{12}y_{16}+y_{17}y_{18}y_{2}+y_{2}y_{24}y_{5},

\displaystyle y_{13}y_{21}+y_{15}y_{29}+y_{16}y_{8},

\displaystyle-y_{1}y_{24}+y_{12}y_{21}-y_{17}y_{3},

\displaystyle y_{12}y_{29}-y_{17}y_{2}y_{7}+y_{19}y_{2}y_{24},

\displaystyle y_{1}y_{7}+y_{12}y_{8}+y_{19}y_{3},

(88)

\displaystyle-y_{13}y_{3}-y_{15}y_{2}y_{7}+y_{18}y_{2}y_{8},

\displaystyle y_{1}y_{29}+y_{17}y_{2}y_{8}+y_{19}y_{2}y_{21},

\displaystyle y_{15}y_{2}y_{24}+y_{16}y_{3}+y_{18}y_{2}y_{21},

\displaystyle y_{12}y_{13}+y_{18}y_{19}y_{2}+y_{2}y_{5}y_{7},

\displaystyle-y_{1}y_{13}+y_{15}y_{19}y_{2}+y_{2}y_{5}y_{8},

\displaystyle y_{1}y_{18}+y_{12}y_{15}-y_{3}y_{5}).

Finally the map

\displaystyle y_{1}\mapsto-p_{0,1},\ y_{3}\mapsto p_{0,4},\ y_{5}\mapsto-p_{1,2},\ y_{7}\mapsto p_{4,5},\ y_{8}\mapsto\frac{p_{0,5}}{y_{2}},\ y_{12}\mapsto-p_{1,4}y_{2},

\displaystyle y_{13}\mapsto-p_{2,5},\ y_{15}\mapsto\frac{p_{0,2}}{y_{2}},\ y_{16}\mapsto p_{2,3},\ y_{17}\mapsto-p_{1,3},\ y_{18}\mapsto-p_{2,4},\ y_{19}\mapsto p_{1,5},

(89)

\displaystyle y_{21}\mapsto\frac{p_{0,3}}{y_{2}},\ y_{24}\mapsto p_{3,4},\ y_{29}\mapsto p_{3,5}

transforms the ideal
(\ref{eq-jacF1231-invert-y2})
into the Plücker ideal (
4.3.1
). Similarly we can show if one of the coordinates
y_{5},y_{6},y_{7},y_{10},y_{19}
does not vanish, then the corresponding open locus is an open subset of a trivial affine fibration over the cone
\widehat{G}(2,6)
.

The proof is completed.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

The ring
S

is normal and Gorenstein.

Proof.

The dimension of
S
is 16.
With the help of Macaulay2, we find a regular sequence of length 16

\displaystyle{y}_{1},{y}_{2},{y}_{3},{y}_{5}+{y}_{21},{y}_{6}+{y}_{24},{y}_{7},{y}_{8},{y}_{10}+{y}_{21},{y}_{13},{y}_{17},{y}_{18}+{y}_{19}+{y}_{27},{y}_{9}+{y}_{11}+{y}_{14},{y}_{20}+{y}_{24},

\displaystyle{y}_{4}+{y}_{7}+{y}_{15}+{y}_{16},{y}_{12}+{y}_{14}+{y}_{15}+{y}_{18}+{y}_{19},-{y}_{7}+{y}_{11}+{y}_{14}-{y}_{15}-2{y}_{19}-2{y}_{29}.

One easily checks that the vector
(-1,-1,-2)
has positive inner products with each weight of the Haiman coordinates
x_{1},\dots,x_{29}
(see Table (
4.3.4
)). So these weights lie in a strictly convex cone.
Applying Theorem
2.15
and Remark
5.11
,
R
is Gorenstein, and so is
S
.

By Proposition
6.2
,
S
is regular in codimension 1. Thus
S
is normal. The proof is completed.
∎

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
X
be the smooth quasi-projective 3-fold. Then

(i)

\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(X)
is normal, Gorenstein for
n\leq 7
, and and has only rational singularities for
n\leq 6
.

(ii)

Let
\rho:\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(X)\rightarrow X^{(n)}
be the Hilbert-Chow morphism. Then for
n\leq 6
,
R^{0}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(X)}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{(n)}}
and
R^{i}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(X)}=0
for
i>0
.

Proof.

Being Gorenstein, and having only rational singularities, are both étale-local properties. Being simultaneously normal and Cohen-Macaulay is also an étale-local property by Serre’s criterion. Since
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(X)
has the same étale-local structure as
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})

, the conclusion (i) for arbitrary smooth quasi-projective 3-folds follows from Proposition
6.1

and Proposition
6.3
. Since
X^{(7)}
has only rational singularities, (ii) follows from (i).
∎

This suggests that, if Conjecture
5.14
is true, one expects that the equality holds moreover in the derived category, so that the local results glue.

Remark 6.5
.

The locus of points of extra dimension 8 in
\mathrm{Hilb}^{7}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
is a trivial
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle over the diagonal
\Delta\cong\mathbb{A}^{3}\xrightarrow{\delta}(\mathbb{A}^{3})^{(7)}
via the Hilbert-Chow morphism
\rho
, where
\delta
is the diagonal embedding. This can be shown by an explicit comparison of
\mathrm{Spec}\ \Bbbk[y_{22},y_{23},y_{25},y_{26},y_{28}]
and the corresponding fiber induced by permuting the coordinates
X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}
on
\mathbb{A}^{3}
. We leave the details to the reader.

Remark 6.6
.

According to the discussions in Section
4.5
, the points with extra dimension 8 on
\mathrm{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{A}^{3})
are expected to be the second simplest singularities. They deserve a detailed study. We do not know whether the point
0\in\mathrm{Spec}(S)
is a rational singularity, though we expect this. Let
f:Y\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(S)
be a resolution of singularities. Having known that
S
is Cohen-Macaulay and the points away from
0
are at most rational singularities, by
[
Kov99
, Lemma 3.3]
the point
0\in\mathrm{Spec}(S)
is a rational singularity if and only if
R^{15}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}=0
. Since the locus of such singularities in
\mathrm{Hilb}^{7}(\mathbb{P}^{3})
is a trivial
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle over the diagonal in
(\mathbb{P}^{3})^{(7)}
, it seems plausible that the Euler characteristic

\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Hilb}^{7}(\mathbb{P}^{3})})
determines
R^{15}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
. But to implement this idea we need to know, not only the locus of points extra dimension 8, but also the structure of an open neighborhood of this locus.

Remark 6.7
.

Proposition
6.2
and its proof give us a way to resolve the singularity of
\mathrm{Spec}(S)
.
Consider the blow-up of
\mathrm{Spec}(S)
at 0, i.e. along
\mathfrak{m}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{21},y_{24},y_{27},y_{29})
.
Denote the homogeneous coordinates corresponding to the generators
y_{1},\dots,y_{21},y_{24},y_{27},y_{29}
of

by
z_{1},\dots,z_{21},z_{24},z_{27},z_{29}
.

We are going to see that the proof of Proposition
6.2
gives all the data of this blow-up. For example, the chart
\{z_{1}\neq 0\}
is smooth. The chart
\{z_{2}\neq 0\}
, after the change of variables (compare to (

\displaystyle z_{21}\mapsto z_{21}+z_{14}z_{27},

\displaystyle z_{24}\mapsto z_{24}+z_{10}z_{14},

becomes
